**2026-08-05 -- Milestone 9G.1: the prepare/commit attack-eligibility predicate was extracted into ONE shared pure function (`evaluateAttackReadiness`), not left as two independently-maintained copies with a parity test bolted on**

Decision: `droneAiStateMachine.ts` gains one new exported pure function, `evaluateAttackReadiness()`, called by BOTH the real attack block inside `decideLegacyDroneAi` (replacing its own previously-inline two-`if` boolean expressions) AND `DroneEnemy.tsx`'s `prepareAttackRequest()` (replacing its own previously-duplicated boolean expression). The extraction was verified byte-identical to the pre-extraction code via the FULL existing `droneAiStateMachine.test.ts`/`droneAiLegacyParity.test.ts`/`droneAiMovementParity.test.ts` suites (zero regressions) plus a new 192-case exhaustive combinatorial test (`state x stunned x canSeePlayer x recoveryBlocksAttack x reactionReady x cooldownElapsed`) against an independently-structured (early-return, not AND-chain) reference oracle.

Reason: 9G's own first pass disclosed `prepareAttackRequest()`'s hand-duplicated predicate as a "future drift risk," and this closure brief's own "Preferred correction" explicitly asked for the shared-predicate extraction over the fallback parity-suite-only option, gated on "only do this when it can be achieved surgically without redesigning the state machine." The extraction WAS surgical (a pure refactor of existing boolean-expression SHAPE, zero change to `DroneAiRuntimeState`, zero change to RNG-consumption order/count, zero change to any timing constant) and is now STRUCTURALLY impossible to drift, since both call sites share one implementation rather than two that merely happen to agree today.

**A real bug was found and fixed during the extraction itself, caught by the project's own existing permanent regression test, not invented for this pass**: the first version of `evaluateAttackReadiness()` unconditionally required `reactionReady` in its AND-chain regardless of state, which broke `droneAiStateMachine.test.ts`'s own pre-existing "reaction gate does not block windup COMPLETION/firing" test (forces a stale/never-satisfied `reactionReadyAtMs` onto an already-`attacking` runtime and confirms firing still occurs) -- the legacy code never re-checks `reactionReady`/`cooldownElapsed` once a windup is already in progress, only when a NEW one is about to start from `engaging`. Fixed by folding the state check in as `(state === 'attacking' || (reactionReady && cooldownElapsed))`, so neither gate is evaluated at all once already `attacking` -- restoring the original test to green and matching the legacy structure exactly, confirmed by re-deriving the equivalence: for an already-`attacking` drone the corrected predicate reduces to exactly `!stunned && canSeePlayer && !recoveryBlocksAttack`, byte-identical to the pre-extraction abort condition.

Rejected: the brief's own fallback (an exhaustive parity suite comparing two independently-hand-duplicated predicates) -- explicitly deprioritized by the brief itself ("Preferred correction: extract... Only do this when it can be achieved surgically"), and inferior even as a fallback: two independently-maintained implementations can still drift on a FUTURE edit to either call site, whereas a shared function structurally cannot. Rejected: leaving the first (buggy) unconditional-`reactionReady` version in place after finding the regression -- would have shipped a real, if narrow, behavior change (an already-attacking drone with a corrupted future `reactionReadyAtMs` would silently drop its shot, a worse failure mode than the original code's robustness to that scenario).

Chosen: the single shared `evaluateAttackReadiness()` function, consumed by both call sites, proven byte-identical via the full existing test suite plus 192 new exhaustive combinatorial cases plus a structural guard test confirming `DroneEnemy.tsx` actually calls the shared function (source-text-anchored) rather than reintroducing a parallel inline predicate.

---

**2026-08-05 -- Milestone 9G.1: live stun-abort observation via the real production damage path was attempted exhaustively and could not be achieved in this session -- precisely root-caused to a measured environment timing constraint, not a methodology gap**

Decision: after confirming real pointer lock is unavailable in this headless session (re-verified fresh via three independent methods -- `page.mouse.click()`, `page.click()`, and raw CDP `Input.dispatchMouseEvent` -- all leave `document.pointerLockElement` null, extending 9F.1's own finding from player movement to weapon aiming), a `pointerLockElement` GETTER override (a browser-API precondition patch, not a game-state/hit-field mutation) combined with continuous real-position pinning of a target drone into the camera's actual, unoverridden forward line let the ENTIRE real, unmodified `VortexFireSystem.tsx` fire pipeline run for real (confirmed via visible ammo consumption and real `hp`/`destroyedAt` mutations the harness never touched directly). Real damage and real destruction were both successfully achieved this way (see the Section 8 death-release trace). Live STUN specifically was not: a direct measurement (recording the real gap between consecutive DroneSquad substeps around a landed hit) found a real inter-frame gap of up to 908ms in this session, consistent with the ~600-780ms independently measured in the 9E.2 entry above -- systematically wider than the 90ms `hitFlashUntil` window the stun mechanic depends on (`flashing = now < hitFlashUntilMs`), so `flashing` essentially never evaluates true by the time `DroneEnemy`'s own next tick checks it, regardless of which AI state the drone was in when hit.

Reason: this is the same class of environment constraint 9E.2 already disclosed for the acquire/reaction/cooldown telegraph windows (170-180ms, also narrower than this environment's real frame gaps), now shown to affect a DIFFERENT, narrower window (90ms) via a DIFFERENT mechanism (a real damage hit, not a state-machine timer) -- confirming the constraint is a genuine property of this specific headless rendering environment's own frame pacing, not an artifact of any one timing window or code path.

Rejected: falling back to directly mutating `hitFlashUntil`/`stunnedUntilMs` to manufacture a stunned state -- explicitly, repeatedly disallowed by this closure brief ("Do not directly mutate hit or stun fields... Direct runtime mutation is not sufficient"), a stricter bar than prior milestones' own accepted "real production mutation contract" fallback, and honored as written rather than quietly reverted to. Rejected: claiming the stun-abort code path was "live-validated" on the strength of the ADJACENT LOS-abort and recovery-abort traces (both of which DO exercise the exact same `abortWindup`/lease-release branch, just via different trigger conditions) -- the brief specifically asked for the STUN trigger itself, and reporting a different trigger's success as satisfying it would misrepresent what was actually observed.

Chosen: report real damage/destruction as achieved via the real production path (Section 8), report live stun specifically as not achieved with the exact measured root cause disclosed here, and note that the stunned-abort CODE PATH itself (as opposed to the live real-hit trigger) is exhaustively covered by existing deterministic tests, including the new 192-case `evaluateAttackReadiness` combinatorial suite, which explicitly includes `stunned=true` as one of its six proven dimensions.

---
